11.2 Licence rules: transfers, ownership changes, renewal, caps, relocation, surrender/revocation, insurance/bonding

11.2.1 Constitutional transfer bar (verbatim)

Article XIV, §2.3(10) (via FindLaw text; Revisor pinpoint Verify): "No marijuana facility, or entity with a certification, shall assign, sell, give, lease, sublicense, or otherwise transfer its license or certificate to any other entity without the express consent of the department, not to be unreasonably withheld." Source: https://codes.findlaw.com/mo/missouri-constitution-of-1945/mo-const-art-14-sect-2/ — accessed 30 October 2026.

  • Practical consequence, confirmed in DCR guidance and practitioner summaries: in Missouri you do not sell the licence as a standalone asset; you sell/transfer the entity that holds it, and DCR must approve the change BEFORE it takes effect. Closing first and filing later risks suspension/revocation; the licensee of record remains responsible until approval. Source (DCR business-change instructions): https://Health.Mo.Gov/safety/cannabis/pdf/full-asset-transfer-to-different-entity-transferee-checklist.pdf — accessed 30 October 2026; and (secondary) https://www.cannabisindustrylawyer.com/how-to-transfer-a-missouri-cannabis-license/ — accessed 30 October 2026.
  • DCR may deny a transfer if not unreasonable under the circumstances (e.g. facility suspended, under investigation, or ownership litigation). False/misleading application information may lead to penalties up to revocation. Source: same DCR checklist PDF, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Document production: under 19 CSR 100-1.030(2)(B)3, licensees must produce documents/records requested in an inspection/investigation within 7 days unless more time is requested and granted; failure may result in a fine of up to $5,000 per day thereafter. Source: same DCR checklist PDF, accessed 30 October 2026.

11.2.2 Business-change applications — marijuana facilities, 19 CSR 100-1.100(2)

Justia text current through Register Vol. 50, No. 12, 16 June 2025. Source: https://regulations.justia.com/states/missouri/title-19/division-100/chapter-1/section-19-csr-100-1-100/ — accessed 30 October 2026.

Change (marijuana facilities)DCR decision clock (from confirmation of COMPLETE application)Rule fee in rule textFY2026–27 fee-schedule figure (see note)
Transfer licence to a different entity with SAME ownership — 100-1.100(2)(A)60 days$2,000$2,000 (comprehensive/medical); microbusiness $1,000
Individual becomes an owner who was not previously an owner — 100-1.100(2)(B)90 days$5,000, assessed once where multiple entities with identical ownership make the same change at the same time$5,000 (facility licensees excl. microbusiness, for 10%+ change by a person who did not previously own 10%+); microbusiness $2,500
Overall change in ownership interests of 50% or more from last approved ownership, incl. full asset transfer — 100-1.100(2)(C)150 days; may ONLY be submitted AFTER the facility has received approval to operate$8,000, assessed once on the same identical-ownership condition$8,000; microbusiness $4,000
Change of facility or warehouse location — 100-1.100(2)(D)90 days$5,000$5,000; microbusiness $2,500
  • Fee-schedule source: DCR Fee Schedule, 1 July 2026–30 June 2027, https://health.mo.gov/business-professionals/cannabis-regulation/fee-schedule — accessed 30 October 2026. Note: the fee schedule labels the $5,000 tier as a 10%-or-more change; the rule text at (2)(B) is framed as "an individual becoming an owner who was not previously an owner" without a 10% threshold in the paragraph opened. Verify whether the fee-schedule 10% gloss reflects a separate DCR interpretation or a later rule amendment before final HTML.
  • Microbusiness discount (verbatim rule): "Any administrative and processing fee for a microbusiness change application shall be half the amount listed in (A)-(D) of this section." Source: 100-1.100(2)(E), same Justia page, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Approval standard (verbatim): "Change applications will be approved if the request contains all of the documents, fees, and information required by this section, and the resulting change in ownership or ownership interests does not violate any provision of this chapter or Article XIV. Change requests will be denied if [either condition fails]." Source: 100-1.100(2)(F), same page, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Full-asset-transfer exhibits (50%+ path): asset purchase agreement; merger/sale/transfer/MOU or like agreement between licensee and transferee; brand, management, consultant or any other agreements/contracts; location lease or proof of ownership; ownership charts (current vs proposed, visual structure incl. all owner entities); verification of no substantially common control between a testing licensee and any other licensee; fingerprint attestation (all proposed owners to submit fingerprints within 2 weeks of submission or have previously submitted, for MSHP state+federal check); for microbusinesses, eligibility documentation. Source: 100-1.100(2)(C), same page, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Medical facilities, 100-1.100(3): parallel change paths (same-ownership entity transfer $2,000; 50%+ $8,000, only after approval to operate; location change $5,000) with the SAME documentation burden but NO stated decision deadline in the rule text opened. Do not import the 60/90/150-day clocks into the medical track. Source: 100-1.100(3), same page, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Management contracts: "If a licensee enters into a contract with a management company or other entity to run all or part of the regulated marijuana operations under this chapter, the contract must permit the licensee to access the licensee-related records of the management company or other entity at the request of the department during an investigation or inspection." Source: 100-1.100(4)(N), same page, accessed 30 October 2026. A buyer taking hiring/firing, bank-account, pricing or revenue control before approval is treated as an unapproved transfer in DCR practice — practitioner summary, Verify against DCR guidance text: https://www.cannabisindustrylawyer.com/management-services-agreement/ — accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Annual ownership reporting: licensees must report at least annually all owners with ownership percentage (marijuana facilities) / all entities owning any part (medical facilities). Source: 100-1.100(6)(B), same page, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Loss of control: "In the event a licensee loses control of their approved location, facility, or license, the license shall be suspended or restricted until a new location is approved or access to the facility or license is restored." Source: 100-1.100(4)(F), same page, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Ownership-dispute suspension: if ownership is disputed to an extent impairing operations or department review, the department may restrict/suspend until resolved or deny a pending application; if restricted/suspended for longer than 1 year on this ground, it may revoke or pursue other remedies. Source: 19 CSR 100-1.070(1)(H), https://regulations.justia.com/states/missouri/title-19/division-100/chapter-1/section-19-csr-100-1-070/ — accessed 30 October 2026.

11.2.3 Microbusiness transfer restrictions — current law vs proposed change

  • Current law (rule text opened): a microbusiness change application must include, where the change affects eligibility, "documentation sufficient to demonstrate eligibility for microbusiness facility ownership, as provided in the application and selection section of this chapter" — i.e. the incoming majority owners must themselves satisfy the eligibility criteria in 19 CSR 100-1.060 (Run 1: net worth/income, VA disability, marijuana arrest, distressed ZIP/tract, unaccredited district). A buyer who meets none of the criteria cannot take majority control. Source: 100-1.100(2)(B)6, (2)(C)7, same 100-1.100 Justia page, accessed 30 October 2026; eligibility summary source (secondary): https://www.illinoislawyernow.com/2026/09/missouri-cannabis-residency-requirement-it-was-repealed/ — accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Separate structural bar: "An owner of a marijuana microbusiness facility may not also be an owner of another licensed marijuana facility or medical facility." Source: 100-1.070(1)(G), Justia 100-1.070 page, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Separate trading bar (from Run 1 / secondary reporting, Verify against 100-1.190 text before final): microbusiness licensees are limited in purchasing/selling with other licence types and cannot freely transfer product to/from comprehensive licensees. Do not state the precise microbusiness-to-microbusiness-only trading rule as verified until 100-1.190 is opened verbatim.
  • Proposed change — NOT law: the 1 October 2026 DHSS package would "establish[] a framework for transferring microbusiness licenses to eligible family members" (DHSS summary). Comments 2 November–2 December 2026; final rule expected 2027. Until a final rule is adopted, there is NO family-transfer pathway in the rule text opened. Source (official): https://content.govdelivery.com/accounts/MODHSS/bulletins/42d722c — accessed 30 October 2026.
  • The same 1 October 2026 package would REMOVE the preapproval requirement for a 50% ownership change and create a framework for publicly traded company ownership. Both are PROPOSED only. Source: same DHSS bulletin, accessed 30 October 2026. Do not describe the 50% preapproval rule as abolished.
  • Revocation context (carried from Run 1, unresolved): Round 2 revocations 25 on 14 April 2025 (24 ownership + 1 felony; 32 notices of pending revocation October 2024); cumulative secondary May 2026 figure 38 revoked + 1 surrendered of 106 issued does NOT reconcile with 9 + 25 = 34 or with Round 3 "~20 freed". This draft does NOT reconcile them. Source: Run 1 report, accessed 30 October 2026.

11.2.4 Ownership caps — Run 1 "3/3/5" NOT confirmed; operative rule found is 10%

  • CONFLICT FLAG — Run 1 figure not adopted. Run 1 notes record ownership caps "3/3/5" as VERIFY. That 3/3/5 formulation was NOT located in the 19 CSR 100-1.070 text opened in this pass, nor in Article XIV §2.3(9)–(11) summaries opened. Do NOT publish 3/3/5 as the cap.
  • Operative rule found (verbatim), 19 CSR 100-1.070(1)(E): "An entity, which includes an individual, may not be an owner in more than ten percent (10%) of the total number of comprehensive and medical licenses, within a facility type, as described in Article XIV Section 2.3(9-11)." Source: https://regulations.justia.com/states/missouri/title-19/division-100/chapter-1/section-19-csr-100-1-070/ — accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Corroboration (secondary, 2026 litigation reporting): a Jackson County Circuit Court class action by VIBE and Local Cannabis alleges the Good Day Farm network controls at least 61 of the state's 224 dispensaries, above the 10% ownership cap. That allegation is consistent with a percentage cap, not a 3/3/5 count cap, but it is an allegation, not a finding. Source (secondary): https://www.cannbus.org/cannmir/mo_cannmir.html (Q3 2026 report) — accessed 30 October 2026. Verify the docket and the Article XIV §2.3(9)–(11) text on revisor.mo.gov before final HTML; if §2.3(9)–(11) contains count caps that the rule paraphrases as 10%, reconcile explicitly — do not silently replace one with the other.
  • Other ownership bars found in the same rule: no facility may be owned in whole or part by an individual with a disqualifying felony offence (see §11.8); no licensee may be owned by/affiliated with an entity holding a Missouri state contract for any product/service related to the department's marijuana programme; testing facilities may not share substantially common control/ownership/management with cultivation, manufacturing or dispensary facilities (see §11.1.6). Source: 100-1.070(1)(A),(B),(D),(F), same page, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Residency: NO Missouri-citizen majority-ownership requirement appears in the current Article XIV text; the former one-year Missouri-citizen majority rule was permanently enjoined in federal court in 2021 (Toigo v. DHSS, per secondary reporting). Out-of-state buyers are not disqualified by residency alone. Source (secondary): https://www.illinoislawyernow.com/2026/09/missouri-cannabis-residency-requirement-it-was-repealed/ — accessed 30 October 2026. Verify against current Revisor text (the 2018 §1 text still circulating in old bills DOES contain the citizen-majority clause — do not quote old bill text as current law).

11.2.5 Renewal requirements and timelines

  • Facility licences: valid 3 years from issuance, renewable except for good cause (constitutional text for cultivation; same 3-year pattern applied across facility types). Source: Article XIV §2.3(9) via https://revisor.mo.gov/main/OneSection.aspx?section=XIV%20Section%202&bid=51541 — accessed 30 October 2026 (Revisor page; pinpoint Verify — URL as returned by search).
  • DCR fee-schedule rule (official, current): "Most marijuana and medical licenses and certifications are valid for three years. Every third year, licensees must submit an application for license renewal, along with the accompanying fee(s), which must be submitted at least 30 days prior to expiration, but no sooner than 90 days. Licensees will receive a notice from the online registry portal reminding them when renewal applications and accompanying fees are due. Renewal applications and fees are not required for seed-to-sale certifications, which have no expiration." Source: https://health.mo.gov/business-professionals/cannabis-regulation/fee-schedule — accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Annual fee still due in the renewal year (Run 1 child report, carried): first annual fee due 30 days after issuance, then annually on the same date while valid; microbusiness first annual fee on the licensure anniversary. Source: 100-1.100(1)(B), Justia 100-1.100 page, accessed 30 October 2026; Run 1 report, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Approval-to-operate clock: approval to operate within 1 year of licensure/certification, 2 years for microbusinesses; absent a granted waiver/variance, a licence may be revoked or sanctioned if not operational and active within that window. Source: 100-1.100(4)(E), same page, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Commencement inspection: licensees must request and pass a commencement inspection before beginning operations under a new licence/certification or at a new location (including new space/vehicles). Source: DCR checklists, e.g. https://health.mo.gov/sites/health/files/media/pdf/2026/03/new-license-location-dispensary-10-25.pdf — accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Verify: the 19 CSR 100-1.100 text opened contains NO standalone "renewal" subsection setting renewal eligibility criteria, good-cause grounds, or late-renewal grace periods. Whether renewal criteria sit in 100-1.020, 100-1.060, or DCR portal guidance was not established in this pass — flag for parent, do not invent a grace period.
  • Agent-ID renewal is DIFFERENT (see §11.8): at least 30 days before expiry, no sooner than 60 days — do not conflate with the facility 90/30-day window.

11.2.6 Relocation

  • Dispensary/microbusiness geographic bar (verbatim): "A dispensary or microbusiness will only be approved to relocate within the congressional district in which they were originally licensed." Source: 100-1.100(1)(D), Justia 100-1.100 page, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Relocation application (marijuana facilities): blueprints of the entire proposed facility (all rooms labelled with purpose/square footage, camera locations, limited-access areas, access permissions); local-government documentation confirming compliance with local distance requirements (or stating none exist); local zoning compliance documentation where applicable; lease or proof of ownership; $5,000 fee ($2,500 microbusiness); 90-day decision clock from complete application. Source: 100-1.100(2)(D), same page, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Buffer at relocation: no facility (incl. offsite warehouses) within 1,000 ft of a then-existing elementary/secondary school, daycare, or church at time of application for licence/certification/local zoning approval (whichever earliest) or relocation, unless expressly allowed by the local government; measurement method governed by Article XIV. Source: 100-1.100(1)(C), same page, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • After approval: the licensee must request a commencement inspection. Source: 100-1.100(6)(I), same page, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Cultivation-practice change is a notification duty, not a relocation: cultivation licensees must notify the department BEFORE changing cultivation practice (indoor/outdoor/greenhouse) or modifying practice ratios. Source: 100-1.100(6)(H), same page, accessed 30 October 2026.

11.2.7 Surrender, suspension, revocation, penalties, appeal

  • No standalone "surrender" procedure was located in the 100-1.100 text opened. Surrenders are visible in practice (e.g. the secondary "38 revoked + 1 surrendered" microbusiness figure, and Round 3 licences freed by "revocations/surrenders" in Run 1), but the surrender form, inventory-disposition steps, and fee consequences are Unavailable in sources opened — flag, do not invent.
  • Enforcement ladder (DCR Path to Compliance Guide, August 2026, official): DCR may limit/restrict operations, impose fines, suspend, non-renew, or revoke; credible imminent threat to health/public safety may support immediate orders; licensees may appeal penalties to the Administrative Hearing Commission (AHC). Source: https://health.mo.gov/sites/health/files/media/pdf/2026/08/Path%20to%20Compliance%20Guide_2.pdf — accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Fine anchors found: failure to report required information — up to $10,000, suspension, or revocation (100-1.100(6)); sampling manipulation — up to $100,000 + suspension/revocation (100-1.110(6)(H)); document-production delay — up to $5,000 per day (100-1.030 via DCR checklist); extraction with combustible gases/dangerous materials without a manufacturing licence — $10,000 penalty (100-1.100(4)(H)); ISO non-compliance — up to $1,000 per day (100-1.110(3)(B)). General penalty ceiling in DCR workshop material: fines up to average daily gross receipts of the previous calendar month (100-1.020(3)) — Verify against 100-1.020 text before final, as this pass relied on a workshop summary. Source: https://health.mo.gov/sites/health/files/media/pdf/2026/03/licensee-workshops-070824.pdf — accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Suspension triggers expressly in rule text: loss of control of location/facility/licence (100-1.100(4)(F)); ownership dispute impairing operations/review (100-1.070(1)(H)); failure to receive approval to operate within 1 year / 2 years microbusiness absent waiver (100-1.100(4)(E)). Sources: Justia pages above, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Recall non-compliance is itself a violation: all facilities are responsible for complying with recall notices; recalled items must be immediately pulled and quarantined. Source: 100-1.100(4)(P), Justia 100-1.100 page, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • AHC jurisdiction (official chart, September 2026): DHSS/DCR facility licences, agent IDs — penalties, revocations, suspensions, denials — are within AHC authority under Mo. Const. art. XIV §§1.3(23), 2.4(27) and 19 CSR 100-1.020(4). Source: https://ahc.mo.gov/wp-content/uploads/2022/07/authority-chart-pdf.pdf — accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Unavailable: no consolidated public disciplinary register was found in Run 1 (Path to Compliance Guide is process, not a register) and none was located in this pass. Individual revocations (e.g. microbusiness rounds) are therefore reported case-by-case, not from a register.

11.2.8 Insurance and bonding minimums

  • Finding: NO cannabis-specific insurance or surety-bond minimum was located in the 19 CSR 100-1 text opened (100-1.070, 100-1.100, 100-1.110) or in Article XIV summaries opened. Do not quote a dollar minimum.
  • What WAS found (secondary, law-firm analysis): "The state of Missouri currently does not have a cannabis business insurance requirement. The only mandated insurance requirement in Missouri for this new industry remains the universal workers compensation insurance requirement, which applies to organizations having five or more employees." And Article XIV §1(13)-type language provides that nothing in the section mandates health-insurance coverage of medical marijuana. Source (secondary): https://heplerbroom.com/blog/one-year-after-legalization-cannabis-and-insurance-coverage-in-missouri-where-weve-been-and-where-were-attempting-to-go/ — accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Caution: practitioner renewal guides that advise "refresh your surety bond and insurance… at or above DCR minimums" (e.g. cannabisindustrylawyer renewal guide) state NO Missouri rule citation for a bond minimum and cite the superseded 19 CSR 30-95 framework in places. Treat any bond figure from such guides as Unverified until a current rule, DCR checklist, or fee-schedule line is produced. Source: https://www.cannabisindustrylawyer.com/missouri-cannabis-license-renewal/ — accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Verify/Unavailable: (a) whether any DCR commencement checklist requires proof of general/product liability insurance as a practical (non-rule) condition; (b) workers' compensation threshold and any cannabis-specific endorsement practice; (c) whether HB 2641 or any 2026 statute added a bond/insurance condition (none found in this pass).

11.3 Cultivation: canopy/plant limits, indoor/outdoor/greenhouse, home grow

11.3.1 Commercial limits — CONFIRMED against 19 CSR 100-1.160

Verbatim substance, 19 CSR 100-1.160(2)(A) (Justia, current through Register Vol. 50, No. 12, 16 June 2025): "Cultivation licensees may cultivate marijuana in indoor, outdoor, or greenhouse facilities or in any combination of these cultivation practices." Source: https://regulations.justia.com/states/missouri/title-19/division-100/chapter-1/section-19-csr-100-1-160/ — accessed 30 October 2026.

PracticeLimit (medical AND comprehensive, per facility)Source, accessed 30 Oct 2026
Indoor (artificial lighting)No more than 30,000 sq ft of flowering plant canopy space100-1.160(2)(A)2, same Justia page
Outdoor (natural lighting)No more than 2,800 flowering plants100-1.160(2)(A)3, same page
Greenhouse (combination natural + artificial lighting)At the ELECTION of the licensee: either no more than 2,800 flowering plants OR no more than 30,000 sq ft of flowering plant canopy space100-1.160(2)(A)4, same page
Combination indoor/outdoor/greenhouseLimited to a ratio of the above limits for each applicable practice, not to exceed 100% of total allowable flowering plant or canopy space100-1.160(2)(A)5, same page
Microbusiness wholesale (any combination)No more than 250 flowering marijuana plants100-1.160(2)(A)1, same page
  • Constitutional parallel (same numbers, framed as "may be limited by the department to…"): Article XIV §1.8 (medical) and §2.3(9) (comprehensive) — indoor 30,000 sq ft; outdoor 2,800 plants; greenhouse election 2,800 plants or 30,000 sq ft; licence valid 3 years, renewable except for good cause. Source: Revisor Article XIV §2 page, accessed 30 October 2026; LawServer §1 text, https://www.lawserver.com/law/state/missouri/missouri-constitution/missouri-constitution-article-xiv-sec-1 — accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Run 1 match: CONFIRMED, no conflict. Run 1's "indoor 30,000 sq ft flowering, outdoor 2,800 plants, greenhouse election, microbusiness wholesale 250 flowering" matches the rule text exactly.
  • Multiple licences, single facility: each facility in operation requires a separate licence, but multiple licences may be utilised in a single facility (subject to department pre-approval under 100-1.100(1)(A)). This is how canopy can stack across licences at one site — do not present a single-facility cap as a single-entity cap. Sources: Article XIV §2.3(9); 100-1.100(1)(A), pages above, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Missouri-grown rule: "All marijuana product sold in Missouri shall have originated from marijuana grown and cultivated in a licensed cultivation facility located in Missouri." Source: 100-1.100(4)(I), Justia 100-1.100 page, accessed 30 October 2026. This is the traceability premise behind the September 2026 GF Extraction recall (chemically modified/synthetic cannabinoids) and the proposed recall-traceability amendments.
  • Cultivation-practice change notification: see §11.2.6 (100-1.100(6)(H)).
  • Verify: detailed cultivation SOP topics in 100-1.160 beyond (2)(A) (e.g. pesticide/fertiliser application records, water, lighting, pest management, harvest handling) were not fully opened in this pass; the DCR cultivation commencement checklist (https://health.mo.gov/sites/health/files/media/pdf/2026/03/new-license-location-cultivation-10-25.pdf — accessed 30 October 2026) should be mined verbatim for the final HTML rather than summarised from secondary guides.

11.3.2 Home grow — cross-reference only (limits NOT changed here)

  • Per Run 1 Part 1 (carried, not re-verified in this pass): registered cardholder 6 flowering + 6 non-flowering (>14 in) + 6 clones (<14 in); household maximum double with two cardholders (12/12/12); consumer cultivation card $115.68 in Run 1 fee reporting (Run 1 flagged a term conflict: 12 months per CSR vs 3 years per fee page — carry that conflict, do not resolve it here). Cultivated product above 3 oz must remain in an enclosed, locked facility at a private residence, not visible from a public place (per secondary summaries of Article XIV; Verify pinpoint before final).
  • No change to home-grow limits is made in Part 11. Any Part 11 statement about "cultivation" without a qualifier means COMMERCIAL licensed cultivation under 100-1.160.

11.4 Security: cameras, alarms, storage, transport

11.4.1 Facility security — 19 CSR 100-1.090

Cornell text of 19 CSR 100-1.090. Source: https://www.law.cornell.edu/regulations/missouri/19-CSR-100-1-090 — accessed 30 October 2026.

  • General duty (100-1.090(1)): licensees shall ensure security of marijuana product and the facility, including any offsite warehouses, by security measures/equipment including: (A) intrusion-detection devices (which may include a signal system interconnected by radio-frequency method such as cellular/private radio, or other mechanical/electronic devices); (B) exterior lighting covering the exterior of all buildings and the facility perimeter (except outdoor cultivation); (C) electronic video monitoring (see below); (F) manual silent alarms at each point-of-sale, reception area, vault, warehouse, and electronic monitoring station, capable of alerting local law enforcement immediately of an unauthorised breach; plus security film or shatter-proof glass on glass doors/storefronts (subsection (G) in the Cornell text as summarised in search results — Verify lettering against full rule text before final, as the search excerpt interleaves subsections).
  • Video (100-1.090(1)(C), verbatim substance): cameras with recording resolution at least 1920×1080p or equivalent, at least 15 frames per second, continuous monitoring allowing identification of people/activities in all lighting levels, capable of being accessed remotely at all times by the department or a law enforcement agency in real time. Motion detection as a method of continuous monitoring is NOT permitted where marijuana product is or will be present. Remote access via https or another department-approved format. Source: same Cornell page, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • DCR interpretation (official FAQ summary, secondary reporting of it): DCR makes one request during the commencement inspection for continuous 24-hour access; thereafter the licensee must provide continuous 24-hour remote-login access without further request. Source (secondary reporting of DCR FAQ): https://stlalarmco.com/cannabis-business-security/ — accessed 30 October 2026. Verify against the DCR Facility FAQs page itself before final.
  • Battery backup: at least 60 minutes for video cameras and recording equipment (100-1.090(1)(C)10 per DCR FAQ summary). Source: same secondary FAQ summary, accessed 30 October 2026. Verify subsection number against full rule text.
  • Retention (secondary summary, widely repeated): 60 days' continuous footage. Source: https://arcdyn.com/solutions/cannabis-industry-security-systems/missouri-cannabis-security-camera-requirements.html — accessed 30 October 2026. Verify against full 100-1.090 text — the Cornell excerpt opened did not itself state the retention period in the lines returned, so do not cite 60 days as rule-verbatim yet.
  • Vaults: "Vaults must be secured in a manner that prevents access to unauthorized individuals through both physical and electronic security measures." Cash and product vaults need NOT be in separate rooms (DCR FAQ: no separation instruction given). Product must be stored in a locked vault or other secure locked enclosure when the facility is not open for business (2023 waiver-history summary; current rule status Verify). Sources: Cornell 100-1.090 page; DCR FAQ summary via https://stlalarmco.com/cannabis-business-security/ — both accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Alarm response: licensees must designate on-call personnel to respond to and be available to law enforcement responding to alarms; automatic/electronic notification + manual silent alarms must alert local law enforcement of an unauthorised breach. Source: Cornell 100-1.090 excerpt, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Outdoor/greenhouse/multi-building: exterior barrier requirements apply (100-1.090(3)); gate to the same standard as the fence (100-1.090(3)(E) per 2023 waiver summary). Full barrier specifications (height, material, etc.) were NOT opened verbatim in this pass — Verify before final; do not invent a fence height.
  • Visibility rule: no cultivation, manufacturing, sale, or display of marijuana product/accessories visible from a public place outside the facility without binoculars, aircraft, or other optical aids. Source: 100-1.100(4)(B), Justia 100-1.100 page, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Licence display: licences displayed within 20 ft of the main entrance at all times. Source: 100-1.100(4)(A), same page, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Consumption on premises prohibited: see §12.3 (100-1.100(4)(M)).

11.4.2 Transport and storage — 19 CSR 100-1.140

Cornell text. Source: https://www.law.cornell.edu/regulations/missouri/19-CSR-100-1-140 — accessed 30 October 2026.

  • Transfer channels only: medical facility → another medical facility or testing facility; comprehensive/microbusiness channels per rule (full channel table NOT fully opened — Verify 100-1.140(2)(B) complete list before final). Testing licensees may only transport product they intend to test. Source: 100-1.140(2)(B)–(C) excerpt, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Transporting agent duties: ensure manifest accuracy; store/transport to prevent contamination/degradation; vehicle must have video monitoring of driver/passenger compartment and any space where product is stored/accessible, plus GPS tracking. Source: 100-1.140(2)(D) excerpt, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Before transport: complete and print an inventory manifest for the trip generated from the statewide track-and-trace system, provided by the originating facility. During transport the agent must: have facility agent ID(s) accessible at all times; have a valid (unexpired) driver's licence accessible; keep a copy of the manifest and trip plan in the vehicle within reach of the driver; have a cell phone or other means to readily communicate with persons/entities outside the vehicle, including law enforcement and the department. Source: 100-1.140(2)(C) excerpt (numbering as returned), accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Recordings/GPS retention: store all video-camera recordings and GPS data for at least 60 days in a secure on-site/off-site location or via a service/network providing on-demand access and copies to the department on request, in the requested format, at licensee expense. Video must be active at all times when product is inside, entering, or exiting the vehicle. Source: same Cornell excerpt, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Dispensary delivery (secondary summary consistent with rule + DCR FAQ): delivery only after DHSS approval/inspection; vehicle unmarked (nothing indicating cannabis transport); product in a secure lockbox/locking cargo area; separate lockbox for cash; 1080p cameras on driver compartment and cargo area; driver carries agent ID, driver's licence, printed Metrc manifest; accident must be reported to law enforcement and the dispensary within 1 hour; driver files a revised trip plan after the route. The 1-hour accident rule and revised-trip-plan rule were NOT verified against the rule text opened in this pass — Verify against full 100-1.140 before final. Source (secondary): https://legalclarity.org/can-dispensaries-deliver-in-missouri/ — accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Storage generally: product stored/transported to prevent contamination/degradation; offsite warehouses are within the facility-security duty (100-1.090(1)) and within the 1,000-ft buffer (100-1.100(1)(C)). Warehouse location changes use the same 90-day/$5,000 business-change path as facility location changes. Sources: pages above, accessed 30 October 2026.

11.5 Waste disposal — 19 CSR 100-1.150

Cornell text. Source: https://www.law.cornell.edu/regulations/missouri/19-CSR-100-1-150 — accessed 30 October 2026.

  • Scope: licensed/certified facilities EXCEPT transportation facilities; covers excess/unusable marijuana waste, unwanted marijuana product, and any waste from the facility. Unused product and solid/liquid wastes from production/processing must be stored, managed and disposed of per applicable state, tribal, local and municipal laws. Source: 100-1.150 purpose + (1) excerpt, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Secure storage before final disposition: within the facility in areas designated for disposal activities, or if necessary outside the facility in a locked, tamper-resistant receptacle. Source: same excerpt, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Hazardous vs non-hazardous split: marijuana flowers, trim, and solid plant material used to create an extract are NOT in themselves hazardous waste unless treated/contaminated with a hazardous-waste constituent; hazardous-waste determination follows 40 CFR 262.11, and the licensee must evaluate and document. Source: same excerpt, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Render-unusable rule (verbatim substance), 100-1.150(5)(D): non-hazardous marijuana product waste "must be rendered unusable prior to leaving a facility" by "grinding and incorporating the marijuana product waste with other nonhazardous ground materials so the resulting mixture is at least fifty percent (50%) nonmarijuana waste by volume. Material used to grind with the marijuana product waste may be either compostable waste or non-compostable waste. Other methods to render marijuana product waste unusable must be approved by the department in writing before implementation." Source: same Cornell page, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Final disposition: rendered-unusable waste may be disposed of at a permitted solid-waste facility; other final-disposition locations require prior written department approval. Source: 100-1.150(5)(D)2, same page, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Commencement-checklist evidence: DCR requires a waste-storage plan (100-1.150(4)), hazardous-waste evaluation per 40 CFR 262.11 (100-1.150(5)), hazardous-waste disposal company identification, rendering-unusable SOPs (100-1.150(5)(A)–(D)), and a waste-disposal contract or department-approved final disposition. Source: DCR dispensary checklist https://health.mo.gov/sites/health/files/media/pdf/2026/03/new-license-location-dispensary-10-25.pdf — accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Records: waste-disposal records fall under the general 5-year record-retention rule (100-1.100(4)(O)) and track-and-trace record duties (100-1.130); the "weekly or when receptacles are full" cadence in vendor guides was NOT found in the rule excerpts opened — Verify, do not state as rule.
  • Solvent-contaminated plant waste: vendor guidance states butane/propane/ethanol-treated material must be off-gassed (e.g. in a C1D1 room) before rendering — secondary only, Verify against rule/DCR guidance: https://www.monarchwaste.co/blog/cannabis-hazardous-waste-vs-non-hazardous — accessed 30 October 2026.

11.6 Environmental: water, pesticide limits, energy

  • Overall finding: Missouri has NO single cannabis-specific environmental code in 19 CSR 100-1 comparable to its testing code. Environmental duties arrive through (a) the pesticide-residue failure limits in testing (§11.1.2), (b) general facility sanitation/environmental-control SOPs, and (c) generally applicable Missouri/federal environmental law (DNR water/wastewater, Department of Agriculture pesticide registration, local sewer authority). Each sub-finding below is labelled accordingly — do not present generic "must conserve water/energy" summaries as Missouri cannabis rules.
  • Pesticide limits (HARD, rule-based): the chemical-residue table in 100-1.110(7)(G)2 IS the operative pesticide limit for product — any listed analyte above its ppm action limit fails the lot (see §11.1.2 for examples). Cultivators must also report in Metrc all pesticides, fertilisers and other chemicals applied, with all ingredients — per NCIA secondary summary; Verify pinpoint in 100-1.130/100-1.160 before final. Source (secondary): https://THECANNABISINDUSTRY.ORG/navigating-missouris-stringent-cannabis-testing-requirements-a-deep-dive/ — accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Pesticide registration: EPA-registered products and Missouri Department of Agriculture Pesticide Program tracking are the registration route for hemp/cannabis pesticides in secondary summaries; Missouri has generally adopted EPA-approved products for hemp. No Missouri list of "cannabis-approved" pesticides was located as a standalone DCR/MDA publication in this pass — Verify/Unavailable for a citable approved-product list. Do not reproduce vendor lists of EPA hemp pesticides as Missouri law.
  • Facility environmental controls (rule-based, general): SOPs must address environmental factors — smooth, hard, easily cleaned floors/walls/ceilings in all areas where marijuana is/will be present; temperature and humidity controls; environmental-condition monitoring; cleaning/sanitising systems and equipment maintenance; and for cultivation/manufacturing facilities, air supply filtered through HEPA filters under positive pressure. Source: 100-1.100(4)(L), Justia 100-1.100 page, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Water/wastewater (generally applicable, NOT cannabis-specific): Missouri Department of Natural Resources (MDNR) guidance for cannabis operators frames the question as NPDES-permit applicability — does the facility generate processed wastewater (water contacting cannabis product/intermediate/finished/byproduct/waste), discharge directly to surface water, have potential to discharge to a nearby waterway/wetland, store wastewater in a basin, use closed-loop/in-house treatment, or discharge to a municipal wastewater facility/POTW (prior authorisation needed, permit may not be needed)? MDNR publication PUB3007, "Wastewater Management for Medical Marijuana Facilities," is the cited guidance. Source (secondary summary of MDNR guidance): https://mogreenway.com/2023/04/18/5-questions-missouri-operators-should-ask-to-determine-stormwater-permit-applicability/ — accessed 30 October 2026. Verify PUB3007 current text and whether it has been updated for adult-use/comprehensive facilities.
  • Water withdrawal: secondary guides assert DNR permits for surface/groundwater withdrawal and water-conservation practices. NO cannabis-specific withdrawal permit threshold was located in rule text opened — Verify/Unavailable; do not state a gallon threshold.
  • Energy: NO energy-use, lighting-efficiency, renewable-energy, or carbon rule for Missouri cannabis facilities was located in 19 CSR 100-1 text opened. Secondary claims that growers are "encouraged" to use energy-efficient practices are aspirational summaries, not rules. Unavailable as a Missouri cannabis requirement. Source checked (secondary, not adopted as rule): https://www.stateregstoday.com/business/cannabis-grower-license-in-missouri — accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Odour, stormwater, air emissions: no Missouri cannabis-specific odour rule was located in this pass; stormwater/NPDES analysis is as above (generally applicable Clean Water Act + MDNR). Local ordinances may impose odour controls — Verify jurisdiction by jurisdiction (Part 3 method), do not generalise.
  • Hemp environmental note (for cross-reference, not Part 11 law): hemp producers are subject to MDA/USDA programme conditions and EPA pesticide registration; hemp-field THC compliance testing and destruction of over-limit crops are programme conditions, not 19 CSR 100-1 rules. See Run 1 Part 7 scope (not this draft).

11.7 Sampling, giveaways, discounts, promotions

11.7.1 Who may sell or give away — dispensaries only

  • DCR guidance (9 February 2023, reported by Greenway): "Dispensary licensees are the only license type with the authority to sell or distribute (give away) marijuana product to qualifying patients, primary caregivers and consumers." Source (secondary report of DCR guidance): https://mogreenway.com/2023/02/15/missouri-regulators-issue-guidance-on-marijuana-samples-and-giveaways/ — accessed 30 October 2026. Verify against the original DCR guidance document before final HTML — the guidance itself was not opened in this pass.
  • Consequence stated in that guidance reporting: "There is no scenario where a licensee or agent ID holder working for a licensee may give away, sell or otherwise distribute marijuana to anyone unless they are doing so in an approved area of the dispensary facility and each individual person and product is entered into Metrc appropriately." Cultivation/manufacturing sales/promotional staff with agent IDs may NOT give samples or gifts outside a dispensary sales floor. Source: same Greenway report, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Rule anchor: dispensary licensees must "Record the disbursement of marijuana product, including plants and seeds, in the state-wide track and trace system, even in instances where prices are discounted or waived." Source: 19 CSR 100-1.180(2)(D)2.F, via https://regulations.justia.com/states/missouri/title-19/division-100/chapter-1/section-19-csr-100-1-180/ — accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Dispensary authority (rule): a dispensary may transport and sell/distribute marijuana product AND drug paraphernalia to another dispensary/manufacturing/cultivation/testing facility, or to individuals authorised to purchase for personal/medical use — medical dispensaries only to qualifying patients/primary caregivers; comprehensive/microbusiness dispensaries to consumers, patients, and caregivers. Source: 100-1.180(1)(A)6, same Justia page, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Pre-rolls: dispensaries may create and sell prerolls, which does NOT include grinding marijuana for preroll use or manufacturing infused products (those remain manufacturing activities). Dispensary-created prerolls are expressly included in mandatory testing (100-1.110(1)(A)). Sources: 100-1.180(1)(A)5; 100-1.110(1)(A), pages above, accessed 30 October 2026.

11.7.2 Discounts, waived prices, and purchase-limit interaction

  • Discounts/waivers are NOT off-the-books: every disbursement, even discounted or waived, is recorded in Metrc (§11.7.1). Whether a $0 giveaway counts against a patient's 30-day purchase limit or a consumer's per-transaction limit depends on the Metrc disbursement entry and the card-scan rule — see below. Do not state that giveaways are "limit-free."
  • Card-scan rule: "For any transaction involving a qualifying patient, primary caregiver, or personal cultivation purchase, scan the department-issued identification card barcode in order to adequately track purchases in the state-wide track and trace system." Source: 100-1.180(2)(D) (lettering as returned), same Justia page, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Payment timing: "Receive payment before the marijuana product leaves the dispensary facility, or, in the case of a delivery order, receive payment at any point in time up until and including the time of delivery." Source: 100-1.180(2)(E) (as returned), same page, accessed 30 October 2026. A $0 giveaway is the limiting case of this rule (no payment to receive) — it still requires Metrc disbursement recording.
  • Public-access control: the general public may only enter through ONE public access point into a screening area where agents screen for patient/caregiver/consumer status; no marijuana product may be accessible in that screening area. Source: 100-1.180(2) excerpt, same page, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Contemporary promotion example (secondary, October 2026 — illustrates that large giveaways are marketed, NOT that DCR pre-approved this one): Stairway Cannabis (Blue Springs and Branson, MO) + LOCAL Cannabis Company "Win a Year of LOCAL" promotion, 1 October–25 November 2026, grand prize 1 lb flower distributed as 4 oz quarterly, winner = most verified qualifying product reviews (not a random drawing). Source (secondary): https://greenwaymagazine.com/2026/10/01/stairway-cannabis-and-local-cannabis-company-team-up-to-give-away-an-entire-pound-of-cannabis-in-missouri/ — accessed 30 October 2026. Verify DCR promotion rules / whether quarterly-instalment fulfilment complies with per-transaction limits before treating this format as compliant — the promotion article does not address limits.
  • Verify/Unavailable: no standalone Missouri rule section titled "promotions," "loyalty programmes," or "discount caps" was located in 100-1.180 excerpts opened. Discount depth, BOGO, loyalty points, and vendor-funded giveaway accounting are therefore NOT stated as capped or uncapped in this draft — flag for parent. What IS rule-verified is: (a) only dispensaries may distribute to patients/consumers; (b) every disbursement incl. discounted/waived must be Metrc-recorded; (c) advertising must comply with 100-1.100(5) (see §11.7.3).

11.7.3 Advertising and promotion limits (rule-verified)

  • 19 CSR 100-1.100(5): advertising/marketing only in compliance with applicable municipal ordinances, state law, and sign/advertising rules. No advertisement may contain: (1) any false/misleading representation; (2) any statement representing marijuana use has curative/therapeutic effects (or tending to create that impression) unless evaluated/approved by FDA; (3) any content attractive to children, including shape/part of shape of an animal or fruit (realistic, artistic, caricature, cartoon renderings) and artistic/caricature/cartoon renderings of shape/part of shape of a human; (4) any statement about a brand inconsistent with its labelling. Outdoor signage, and interior signage visible from a public right of way, must comply with local sign ordinances. Exterior signage/advertising must accurately reflect the licensee's legal/business/d/b/a/trade name on record. Source: 100-1.100(5)(A)–(D), Justia 100-1.100 page, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Dispensary warnings/signage: dispensaries must post at each point of egress, and on/beside/immediately above all drive-through drawers, a department-approved sign conveying: "It is against the law to operate a dangerous device, motor vehicle, aircraft, or motorboat while under the influence of marijuana." Drive-through/pickup windows must use drawers/pneumatic tubes, allow clear visibility for identity verification, remain closed/locked except when in use (construction as returned), and be video-covered to chapter standards. Source: 100-1.180(2) excerpt, Justia 100-1.180 page, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Food-safety overlay: dispensaries selling infused products for oral consumption (incl. tinctures) must ensure storage/handling complies with 19 CSR 20 food-safety standards and relevant food-safety statutes. Source: 100-1.180(2) excerpt, same page, accessed 30 October 2026.

11.7.4 Consumer sampling at the point of sale vs licensee sampling

  • Do not conflate three different "samples": (1) mandatory-testing samples collected by the testing licensee under 100-1.110(5)–(6) (§11.1.3); (2) DCR routine-sampling initiative samples collected by DCR staff during unannounced inspections (§11.1.5); (3) promotional "samples"/giveaways distributed by dispensaries under §11.7.1. Only (3) reaches a consumer, and only via a dispensary with Metrc recording. No rule authorising on-site consumption of a promotional sample at a dispensary was located — and on-premises consumption is prohibited (100-1.100(4)(M); see §12.3). Verify whether any DCR variance permits tasting/sample events; none was found in this pass.

11.8 Drug testing, background checks, agent ID cards for licence holders and staff

11.8.1 Agent ID cards — who needs one, validity, fee, renewal

  • Who (verbatim), 19 CSR 100-1.070(2)(A): "All employees, contractors, owners having access to a medical or marijuana facility, and volunteers of a medical or marijuana facility must obtain an agent identification card from the department before beginning employment, work, or volunteer services at a licensed or certified facility." Source: https://regulations.justia.com/states/missouri/title-19/division-100/chapter-1/section-19-csr-100-1-070/ — accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Maintenance exception: a person performing maintenance (e.g. plumbing) or similar work NOT related to testing, transporting, growing, manufacturing, or dispensing, for no more than 14 days in a calendar year, needs no agent ID; the licensee must supervise such persons in the facility. Source: 100-1.070(2)(A)1, same page, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Age: all facility agents must be 21 or older; persons under 21 holding a card before the rule's effective date may remain agents. Source: 100-1.070(2)(B), same page, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Carry/display: card visible and on the person at all times while working in a facility or on behalf of a licensed/certified entity; government-issued photo ID on the person at all times while the agent card is visible. Source: 100-1.070(2)(C), same page, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Fee (verbatim rule + fee schedule agree): "The department shall charge an administration and processing fee of seventy-five dollars ($75) for identification cards, which shall be due at the time of application or renewal." Source: 100-1.070(2)(I)2, same page, accessed 30 October 2026; DCR fee schedule confirms $75.00 non-refundable: https://health.mo.gov/business-professionals/cannabis-regulation/fee-schedule — accessed 30 October 2026; DCR agent-ID page confirms "$75.00 non-refundable fee": https://health.mo.gov/business-professionals/cannabis-regulation/facility-agent-id-how-apply — accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Validity/renewal (verbatim): "Agent identification cards are valid for three (3) years from their date of issuance and shall be renewable by submitting, prior to expiration by at least thirty (30) days but no sooner than sixty (60) days, a new or renewal application." Source: 100-1.070(2)(F), same page, accessed 30 October 2026. NOTE the different window from facility licences (90/30 days, §11.2.5).
  • Application contents: name, address, Social Security number; government-issued photo ID confirming age over 21; copy of written offer/confirmation of employment from a licensed/certified facility; all applicable fees. Source: 100-1.070(2)(H), same page, accessed 30 October 2026. DCR portal note (current practice): the Written Offer of Employment upload is no longer a substantive requirement under a department waiver, but the portal still requires a document upload in that tab to submit; applicants register as Patient/Caregiver/Physician application type, NOT as Business. Source: DCR agent-ID page above, accessed 30 October 2026. Verify portal status at final, as DCR states it is working to remove the upload requirement.
  • Contact updates: agents must keep contact information current and agree to receive department communications by email (including denials/revocations); name/address/email changes notified within 14 days. Source: 100-1.070(2)(G), same page, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Approval/denial: on a complete application/renewal, the department shall either approve or provide a written explanation for denial. Source: 100-1.070(2)(I), same page, accessed 30 October 2026.

11.8.2 Background checks and disqualifying felony offences — including the September 2026 emergency amendment

  • Constitutional definition, Article XIV §2.3(9)(1) (Revisor text via search; pinpoint Verify): "No owner of a marijuana facility or entity with a transportation certification shall be an individual with a disqualifying felony offense. A 'disqualifying felony offense' is a violation of, and conviction or guilty plea to, state or federal law that is, or would have been, a felony under Missouri law, regardless of the sentence imposed, unless the department determines that: (a) The person's conviction was for a marijuana offense that has been expunged or is currently eligible for expungement under this section; or (b) The person's conviction was for a non-violent crime for which he or she was not incarcerated and that is more than five years old; or (c) More than five years have passed since the person was released from parole or probation, and he or she has not been convicted of any subsequent felony [criminal offenses]." Source: https://revisor.mo.gov/main/OneSection.aspx?section=XIV%20Section%202&bid=51541 — accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Medical parallel, Article XIV §1 (Justia text): disqualifying felony offence defined the same way, with carve-outs for (1) medical use of marijuana / assisting medical use, (2) nonviolent crime, not incarcerated, more than 5 years old, (3) more than 5 years since release from parole/probation with no subsequent criminal offences; department may consult/rely on Attorney General and Department of Public Safety records/advice. Note the medical and adult-use carve-outs DIFFER in wording (medical-use vs expunged/eligible marijuana offence) — do not merge them. Source: https://law.justia.com/constitution/missouri/article-xiv/section-1/ — accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Rule bars: "No medical facility shall be owned, in whole or in part, by an individual with a disqualifying felony offense." / "A marijuana facility shall not have as an owner any individual with a disqualifying felony offense." Owners must notify the department of any felony charges, including case number, within 30 days of being charged. Source: 100-1.070(1)(A)–(C), Justia 100-1.070 page, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Employer background checks: "A licensee may require a criminal background check as a condition of employment." (permissive, employer-level). Source: 100-1.070(2)(D), same page, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Department fingerprint authority (rule, conditional): "If authorized or directed by statute, the department may require fingerprint submission to screen agent identification card applicants for disqualifying felony offenses." Source: 100-1.070(2)(E), same page, accessed 30 October 2026. Statutory authority is §§195.815, 195.817 RSMo (Supp. 2026) per the emergency-amendment authority line.
  • Current fingerprint practice (DCR page): following FBI approval of DCR's statutory authority to review state+federal criminal-history records under 100-1.070(2)(E), DCR RESUMED the fingerprint-submission requirement for agent-ID applicants to screen for disqualifying felony offences. After application, DCR emails a new four-digit fingerprint code + instructions; applicants have 14 days to submit fingerprints; applications are incomplete until fingerprint results are received. Submissions are retained by the state central repository and FBI, searched against fingerprints on file including latent prints, and enrolled in the FBI National Rap Back programme. Distribution of the code/instructions outside the intended recipient is strictly prohibited. Source: DCR agent-ID page above, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Retrospective fingerprinting: DCR required fingerprint submissions from active agent-ID cardholders approved 8 December 2022–1 December 2024 who had not submitted at application, contacted from 3 February 2025 in approval-date order; persons determined to have a disqualifying felony offence, or who failed to submit in time, were subject to revocation under 100-1.070(2)(J), after a pending-revocation notice allowing a further 14 days. Source (official DCR notice): https://health.mo.gov/sites/health/files/media/pdf/2026/03/notice-for-active-agents-508.pdf — accessed 30 October 2026.
  • September 2026 emergency amendment — material, time-limited, must be flagged as such: Emergency amendment to 19 CSR 100-1.070 filed 16 September 2026, effective 30 September 2026, expiring 28 March 2027 (Missouri Register publication 2 November 2026 for the emergency amendment; a proposed rule covering the same + additional material to follow). Purpose: adds prohibition against issuing NEW or RENEWAL agent IDs to individuals with disqualifying felony offences, and clarifies the disqualifying-felony review standard. Emergency statement: on 4 September 2026 the Administrative Hearing Commission found DCR lacks authority in statute/regulation to SCREEN employees for disqualifying felony offences; the effect was that while DCR could require fingerprint submission under §195.817, it could NOT prohibit felons from acquiring an agent ID and working in facilities. The emergency amendment is limited to the circumstances creating the emergency; "no additional screening will occur for current employees as a result of this emergency amendment." Source (official SOS emergency-rule PDF): https://www.sos.mo.gov/CMSImages/AdRules/main/EmergenciesforInternet/19c100-1.070a.pdf — accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Consequence for final HTML: do NOT state flatly that "felons cannot work in Missouri cannabis facilities" without the September 2026 AHC/emergency-amendment qualification. As of 30 October 2026 the position is: (a) owners with disqualifying felony offences are barred (constitutional + rule); (b) for NEW/RENEWAL agent IDs, the emergency amendment (eff. 30 Sept 2026–28 Mar 2027) purports to bar issuance to persons with disqualifying felony offences; (c) current employees are expressly NOT additionally screened under that amendment; (d) the underlying AHC authority question is unresolved pending the proposed/permanent rule. Verify AHC decision citation and permanent-rule status at final.
  • Denial/revocation grounds for agent IDs (100-1.070(2)(J)): incomplete application; deceptive/misleading/incorrect/false/fraudulent information (including by omission/ambiguity); fraudulent use/tampering/duplication/allowing another to use the card; selling/distributing/transferring/giving product to unauthorised persons or in unauthorised amounts; tampering with video, POS, track-and-trace or other facility records; track-and-trace non-compliance; violation of any chapter requirement; being prohibited by law from holding a card; theft/other criminal offence in performance of agent duties (whether or not charged); refusal to cooperate with a department investigation; prior revocation (new application denied absent department finding of good cause). Source: 100-1.070(2)(J), Justia page, accessed 30 October 2026.

11.8.3 Workplace drug testing for facility staff vs general employment (bridge to Part 12)

  • No Missouri rule in 100-1.070 requires licensees to drug-test their own agents as a condition of holding an agent ID. General-employer drug testing in Missouri is governed by the framework in §12.7 (no general state drug-testing statute; medical-cardholder anti-discrimination rule; adult-use users unprotected). Facility agents are employees like any others for that analysis, plus the agent-ID revocation grounds above (which include on-duty misconduct, not a positive test per se). Do not invent a licensee drug-testing mandate.
  • Transport agents have the additional operational duties in §11.4.2 (agent ID accessible, valid driver's licence accessible) — a suspended/revoked agent ID or expired driver's licence during transport is a transport violation, not a drug-test issue.

11.9 Research and university work

11.9.1 The new research-facility licence — 19 CSR 100-1.200 (final rule, effective date VERIFY)

  • Rulemaking history (official): DHSS/DCR filed PROPOSED 19 CSR 100-1.200 on 3 April 2026 (Missouri Register 1 May 2026; comments 1–31 May 2026; final version expected summer 2026). Source (official): https://content.govdelivery.com/accounts/MODHSS/bulletins/41160b8 — accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Final adoption (secondary report of Missouri Register 1 October 2026): final rules adopted after 35 comments, published in the 1 October 2026 Missouri Register. Run 1 reported an effective date of 30 November 2026. This pass did NOT open the final rule text itself — Verify effective date and final text against the Missouri Register / SOS CSR compilation before final HTML. Do not cite 30 November 2026 as confirmed; cite it as "reported effective 30 November 2026 (Run 1) — Verify."
  • Scope as reported (secondary summary of the final rule — Verify against rule text): licensed research facilities may cultivate marijuana, manufacture/process products, store products on-site, conduct human-subject and animal-subject studies, administer product to participants under approved protocols; human studies require review by BOTH the department's Institutional Review Board AND an outside IRB registered with the U.S. Department of Health and Human Services; human administration to adults 21+ and to under-21 participants holding a medical patient ID card; animal studies require USDA animal-welfare research registration + IACUC approval in good standing with NIH Office of Laboratory Animal Welfare; plant/genetics research incl. developing/naming/tracking new strains, cultivars, phenotypes, chemotypes, genetic lines, cultivation variables, production methods; acquire from licensed cultivators/manufacturers, transfer to testing labs/other research facilities, sell/donate to another licensed research facility; transfer research-derived seeds/clones to licensed cultivation facilities under an approved study; acquire seeds from outside Missouri's regulated system where not violating state/federal law; contract with public institutions/other research facilities; share space with licensed cultivation/manufacturing/testing facilities. A research licence alone does NOT authorise studies — the operator must pass a commencement inspection AND receive state approval for a specific study. Application fee reported $500 (non-refundable) + per-study fee (amount cut off in source excerpt — Verify). Source (secondary): https://themarijuanaherald.com/2026/09/missouri-marijuana-research/ — accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Run 1 labelling note (secondary, Verify against rule text): research product tracked in Metrc and labelled "For Research Purposes Only" / "Not For Sale or Distribution." Source: Run 1 report, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • No licensed research facility, approved study, or university holder is named in any official source opened in this pass. Do not name a university as a research licensee.

  • Federal overlay (flag, not Missouri law): marijuana research involving human subjects still engages federal controlled-substance, FDA, and DEA requirements, which are NOT resolved by a Missouri research licence alone. The Run 1 federal baseline (adult-use Schedule I; medical Schedule III only) means a Missouri research licence does not by itself authorise federally unlawful activity. Verify DEA/FDA research pathways separately if the parent wants a federal-research subsection — do not summarise them from memory here.

PART 12 — CONSUMER AND SOCIAL RULES

12.1 Paraphernalia and smoke-shop rules

12.1.1 Criminal paraphernalia offences — Chapter 579 (current statute, Revisor)

Revisor Chapter 579 index. Source: http://revisor.mo.gov/main/OneChapter.aspx?chapter=579 — accessed 30 October 2026.

OffenceStatuteCore elements / penalty as returnedSource, accessed 30 Oct 2026
Unlawful distribution, delivery, or sale of drug paraphernalia§579.040Listed in Chapter 579 index (effective 1 Jan 2017). Full elements/penalty NOT reproduced in the excerpt opened — Verify full text before final.Revisor Chapter 579 index above
Unlawful possession of drug paraphernalia§579.074"A person commits the offense of unlawful possession of drug paraphernalia if he or she knowingly uses, or possesses with intent to use, drug paraphernalia to plant, propagate, cultivate, grow, harvest, manufacture, compound, convert, produce, process, prepare, test, analyze, pack, repack, store, contain, conceal, inject, ingest, inhale, or otherwise introduce into the human body, a controlled substance or an imitation controlled substance in violation of this chapter or chapter 195." Penalty: class D misdemeanor, UNLESS previously found guilty of a controlled-substance offence (Missouri or another jurisdiction), then class A misdemeanor (prior findings pleaded/proven per §558.021); class E felony if paraphernalia used in combination to manufacture/compound/produce/prepare/test/analyse amphetamine/methamphetamine or analogues. Formerly §195.233; transferred 2014; effective 1 Jan 2017.http://revisor.mo.gov/main/ViewChapter.aspx?chapter=579 (Revisor text as returned)
Unlawful manufacture of drug paraphernalia§579.076"A person commits the offense of unlawful manufacture of drug paraphernalia if he or she unlawfully manufactures with intent to deliver drug [paraphernalia…]" (excerpt cut off at that point). Full text/penalty Verify. Formerly related provisions; effective 1 Jan 2017.Same Revisor chapter text
  • Critical qualifier: §579.074 criminalises use/possession "in violation of this chapter or chapter 195." Possession/use of paraphernalia FOR LAWFUL marijuana activity authorised by Article XIV (e.g. a patient/consumer using a pipe for lawfully possessed marijuana) is therefore not on the face of the statute the same offence as paraphernalia for unlawful controlled-substance use — but NO Missouri appellate holding to that effect was located in this pass, and municipal paraphernalia ordinances may differ. Do not state a blanket "paraphernalia is legal in Missouri" rule. State the statutory qualifier + the verification gap.
  • Dispensary paraphernalia authority (rule): dispensaries may package/store (on- or off-site) marijuana product AND drug paraphernalia used to administer marijuana product, and may transport/sell/distribute paraphernalia to other licensees and authorised purchasers (see §11.7.1). Source: 100-1.180(1)(A)5–6, Justia 100-1.180 page, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • HB 2641 naming rule (Run 1, carried): §195.900(8) — only comprehensive/medical/microbusiness dispensaries may use "dispensary" (or similar import) in a business name when selling CBD/hemp/marijuana/cannabinoids/hemp-derived products/paraphernalia. Penalty §195.900(10): $5,000 per transaction + class D felony. Effective 12 November 2026 (see Run 1 baselines). Source: Run 1, accessed 30 October 2026. Verify §195.900(8) pinpoint against enrolled HB 2641 text before final.
  • Smoke shops / non-licensed retailers: NO Missouri statute or rule creating a "smoke shop licence" was located in this pass. Non-licensed sale of paraphernalia sits under §579.040 (distribution/sale) + general business licensing + local ordinances, and — from 12 November 2026 — under HB 2641 §195.900 for hemp-derived cannabinoid products/paraphernalia sold with them. A shop selling only tobacco paraphernalia vs a shop selling items marketed for unlawful drug use are treated differently in enforcement practice (secondary practitioner summaries note the "sold for tobacco use" labelling practice and police/prosecutor focus on actual intended use), but no Missouri rule text blessing a "for tobacco use only" defence was located. Verify local smoke-shop ordinances (e.g. St. Louis City, Kansas City, Springfield) jurisdiction by jurisdiction if the parent wants city rules — do not generalise from one city.
  • Age: dispensary paraphernalia sales follow dispensary purchaser rules (21+ consumers; patients/caregivers per card). A standalone statewide age limit for non-dispensary paraphernalia sales was NOT located in this pass — Verify (§579.040 full text + local ordinances).

12.2 THC driving limits and impairment testing — Missouri has NO per se THC limit

12.2.1 The offence

  • Verbatim (current §577.010(1), Revisor): "A person commits the offense of driving while intoxicated if he or she operates a vehicle while in an intoxicated condition." Source: https://revisor.mo.gov/main/OneSection.aspx?section=577.010&bid=33822 — accessed 30 October 2026.
  • "Intoxicated condition" means under the influence of alcohol, a controlled substance, or drug, or any combination thereof (§577.001 definition as summarised in secondary sources; Verify §577.001 pinpoint). There is NO separate Missouri "DUI" offence — Missouri uses DWI for alcohol and drugs alike. Source (secondary summary of §§577.001/577.010/577.012): https://www.mrdlawyers.com/cases-we-handle/dwi-defense/missouri-dwi-laws/ — accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Penalty tiers, first-offence anchor: first offence class B misdemeanor; class A misdemeanor if prior offender or a person under 17 present in the vehicle; class E felony if persistent offender or criminal negligence causing physical injury; class D felony and above for aggravated/chronic offenders and injury/death cases (full tier table in §577.010(2)). Suspended imposition of sentence restrictions apply (minimum 2 years' probation for first offence, etc.). Source: Revisor §577.010 text above, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Alcohol contrast (to show what a per se limit looks like): §577.012, driving with excessive blood alcohol content — 0.08% or more by weight of alcohol in blood is itself an offence, separate from DWI. No parallel THC section exists. Source: Revisor §577.010 page context + secondary summary above, accessed 30 October 2026. Verify §577.012 text directly before final.

12.2.2 No per se THC limit — verified negatively, with the limits of that verification

  • Finding: NO ng/mL THC blood/urine threshold, and NO "any detectable THC = DWI" per se rule for marijuana, was found in Chapter 577, Article XIV, or 19 CSR 100-1 text opened. Multiple independent secondary sources agree Missouri has no per se THC limit (e.g. "Missouri does not have a per se limit" — Beerup Law; "there's no legal THC limit for cannabis" — multiple defence-firm summaries). The State must prove the driver operated while in an "intoxicated condition" — i.e. impaired — not merely that THC/metabolites were present. Sources (secondary, convergent): https://www.beeruplaw.com/missouri-marijuana-law-and-driving ; https://www.beeruplaw.com/missouri-marijuana-and-drug-dwi ; https://www.combswaterkotte.com/practice-areas/weed-dwi-dui-lawyer-st-louis-mo/ — all accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Do NOT import another state's limit. In particular do NOT quote Illinois's 5 ng/mL delta-9-THC whole-blood / 10 ng/mL other-substance per se rule, Colorado's 5 ng/mL permissible-inference rule, or any other state's figure as Missouri law. Those figures appear in 50-state tables alongside Missouri's impairment-based entry; the Missouri entry in the September 2026 50-state table is impairment-based. Source (secondary 50-state table, 15 Sept 2026): https://themarijuanaherald.com/2026/09/marijuana-dui-laws-by-state/ — accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Practical enforcement (secondary, not rule text): officers rely on driving observations, field sobriety tests, Drug Recognition Expert (DRE) 12-step evaluations, and blood/urine toxicology. Defence-firm summaries stress that THC persists days/weeks after impairment ends, that field tests were not validated for cannabis impairment, and that a 5 ng/mL blood figure is often cited by prosecutors as evidence but is NOT a statutory threshold — and that regular users may exceed 5 ng/mL while sober. Treat all of this as practice commentary, NOT as Missouri law, and do not quote the Nature-study percentages as Missouri findings. Sources: Beerup pages above, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Implied consent / refusal: Missouri's implied-consent law (§§577.020–577.041) deems drivers on public highways to consent to chemical tests (breath/blood/saliva/urine) on arrest on reasonable grounds of DWI; refusal consequences (including licence revocation exposure) are stated in secondary summaries as 1-year revocation — Verify current §§577.020, 577.041 text and DOR practice before final; do not state the refusal penalty as rule-verified here.
  • Dispensary/vehicle warnings (rule): dispensary egress + drive-through signs must warn it is against the law to operate a dangerous device, motor vehicle, aircraft, or motorboat while under the influence of marijuana (see §11.7.3). Consumption in a vehicle on a public road is prohibited (see §12.3).
  • Verify/Unavailable: (a) §577.001 "intoxicated condition" + "controlled substance" definitions pinpoint; (b) whether any Missouri appellate decision has addressed THC-metabolite-only DWI sufficiency (none located in this pass); (c) DOR/DPS (Missouri State Highway Patrol) DRE programme size/training standards as official figures (secondary claims of 80–100 hours' DRE training and "fewer than half of agencies have a DRE" are unverified — do not quote as official); (d) any 2026 DWI bill (e.g. SB 1579, introduced 2026, repealing/re-enacting §577.010 — introduced text only, NOT law; do not present its penalty re-tiers as current).

12.3 Automatic expungement under Article XIV — deadlines, court role, counts as CONFLICT (range, not a picked figure)

12.3.1 Constitutional mechanism

  • Verbatim core (Article XIV §2.10(8)(a), as quoted in Missouri Court of Appeals WD86993, 9 Sept 2025): "Within six months of the effective date of this section, the circuit courts of this state shall order the expungement of the criminal history records of all misdemeanor marijuana offenses for any person who is no longer incarcerated or under the supervision of the department of corrections. Within twelve months of the effective date of this section, the circuit courts [shall order expungement for felony marijuana offences on the terms stated in the following sentences]." Source: https://cases.justia.com/missouri/court-of-appeals/2025-wd86993.pdf?ts=1757431809 — accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Calendar translation (effective date 8 December 2022): misdemeanour deadline 8 June 2023; felony deadline 8 December 2023. Those deadlines were MISSED in practice (paper records, manual processing, record-matching burden) and work has continued since — Run 1 and multiple secondary sources agree on the miss; no source in this pass claims the deadlines were met. Source: Run 1 report, accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Immediate legal effect (Article XIV §2.10(8)(b), as quoted in Lozano SCOTUS appendix, 8th Cir. 27 Mar 2026): "An expungement order shall be legally effective immediately and … the conviction and sentence shall be vacated as legally invalid." Source: https://www.supremecourt.gov/DocketPDF/26/26-5456/422720/20260901164720206_Lozano%20-%20SCOTUS%20-%20Appendix.pdf — accessed 30 October 2026.
  • Petition pathways (secondary summaries, Verify against §2.10(7)–(8) full text): persons still incarcerated/under supervision for a misdemeanour or class E/D felony involving ≤3 lbs marijuana may petition the sentencing court to vacate sentence and order immediate release + expungement, with court adjudication timetables in the text (90/180/270 days by offence class as summarised in secondary sources); larger-quantity felonies (class A/B/C/D, >3 lbs) expunged on completion of supervision. Exclusions in secondary summaries: violence, distribution to a minor, driving under the influence. Do not quote the petition timetables as verbatim until §2.10(7) is opened in full.
  • Court role: expungement is ordered by the CIRCUIT COURTS (not by DCR, not by the Governor, not automatically by a database). The Office of State Courts Administrator (OSCA) and the Missouri State Highway Patrol Criminal Justice Information Services / central repository are the record systems that must implement orders. No updated official OSCA total for 2025/2026 was located in official sources in Run 1 or this pass.
  • Separate petition expungement (statute, NOT Article XIV automatic expungement): §610.140 RSMo petition expungement (waiting periods, lifetime caps — e.g. up to 2 felonies / 3 misdemeanours in secondary summaries) remains a distinct route, including for records missed by the automatic process. Do not merge §610.140 caps into the Article XIV automatic counts. Verify §610.140 current text + any 2025 expungement-reform statute before final (a secondary item claimed a NEW §610.141 automatic-expungement statute for certain drug-possession/paraphernalia convictions, operational when technically feasible and no later than 1 Jan 2027 — that claim was found on a low-authority consumer-news site only and is flagged here as Unverified — do not present as law until confirmed on revisor.mo.gov: https://www.theconsumernewsnetwork.com/agp-article/944623561-missouri-sets-automatic-expungement-for-some-drug-records — accessed 30 October 2026).
